5. FPC shall secure establishment of regional councils. Section 104(a). 

6. Same as 2-Section 4(a). 

7. No provision. 

8. Same as 5. Section 404(a). 

What are the provisions with respect to such councils? 

1(a) Membership:-Must be open to all utilities regardless of nature of ownership; Section 407(a). 

1(b) Voting, costs, and representation:-Each council shall establish method of weighing votes, sharing costs and representation. Section 407(a). 

1(c) Government participation:-FPC's and state commission representatives shall be permitted to participate in council's activities. Section 407(b).

1(d) Mandatory participation:-No provision for mandatory participation by utilities. 

1(e) Information to be furnished:-Each council shall file with FPC a statement of its organization. Section 407(c). (175)

1(f) Antitrust:-Antitrust laws do not preclude participation in council activities but no immunity shall be granted for plans or agreements in violation of antitrust laws. Section 407 (e). 

2. No provisions. 

3(a) Membership:-Same as 1(a). Section 10(a). 

3(b) Voting, costs, and representation:-Same as 1(b). Section 10(a). 

3(c) FPC and state commission participation:-No provision. 

3(d) Mandatory participation:-May be required by FPC. Section 10(d). 

3(e) Information:-Same as 1(e). Section 10(b). 

4(a) Membership:-Same as 1(a). Section 11(a). 

4(b) Voting, costs, and representation: Same as 1(b). Section 11(a). 

4(c) FPC and state commission participation: Same as 1(c). Section 11(b). 

4(d) Mandatory participation: Same as 3(d). Section 11(c). 

4(e) Information:-Same as 1(e). Section 11(c). 

5(a) Membership:-Same as 1(a). Section 104(a). 

5(b) Voting, costs, and representation: No provision. 

5(c) FPC and state commission participation:-FPC's and state commission representatives shall participate in the work of the councils except for the ultimate adoption of plans or any other council actions. Section 104(a). 

5(d) Mandatory participation:-No provision. 

5(e) Information to be furnished:-Same as 1(e). Section 104(b). 

6. No provision. 

7. No provision.

STED 1920 

8. Same as 5 except that antitrust immunity is provided with regard to Section 4 of the Clayton Act if FPC finds that the effect of cooperative activities upon completion will be insubstantial or will be clearly outweighed by other public interest considerations. Section 404. 

How is a regional plan to be developed? 

1. Each regional council shall develop one or more plans and make appropriate arrangements for public agencies to evaluate plans and develop alternatives. 

Section 402 (a) (b). 

Once each year the council shall submit a plan to the FPC and the state commissions describing construction proposals in sufficient detail for the public agencies and others to evaluate. Plan shall show alternatives considered and why rejected and what action were taken by public agencies in the developing, approving and disapproving of particular proposals in the plan. Section 402(c). 

Following publication and receipt of comments the regional council may revise the plan and shall then publish it in final form. Section 402(d). 

2. Each electric entity shall prepare an annual long range plan which may be part of a regional plan that described planned construction and the sites to be used. Efforts to coordinate the facilities with other electric entities shall be described along with efforts made to minimize environmental problems. Plan shall be filed with appropriate Federal, regional, or state certifying agency, with FPC and EPA and with other affected groups. Guidelines for these plans shall be issued by the FPC. Section 4(a). 

The certifying bodies are directed to review long-range plans, comment on them, and make them available to the public; to compile and publish proposed power sites; to conduct mandatory hearings on sites five years in advance of construction; and to provide for public hearings when a certificate for a given site is 

3. At intervals of not more than 2 years each regional council shall propose standards with respect to load forecasting, installed reserves, spinning reserves, etc. and obtain comments from FPC, State Commissions, and the National Council. After informal discussions among these bodies, the FPC shall publish the regional council standards: FPC comments on such standards may be appended. 

If a council fails to prepare standards the FPC will do so. Section 4(d). 

If failure to comply contributes to bulk power failure, FPC may require mandatory rather than voluntary compliance by utilities in the region. Section 4 (e). 

4. Basically the same as 3. Section 5. 

5. FPC shall require submission of coordination plans by the councils in accordance with rules and standards prescribed by FPC. After consideration of

plans and opportunity for public hearings, FPC may modify or set aside any plan which is not in accord with the objectives of the bill. Section 104(c). 

6. Similar to 2. Section 4. 

7. No provision. 

8. Similar to 5. Section 404(b) and (c). 

What period of time must the plan cover? 

1. At least ten years. Section 402(c). 

2. Ten years unless Federal agency requires longer period which is not to exceed 15 years; five years for inventory of sites. Section 4(a). 

3. Ten years. Section 5(c). 

4. Minimum of ten years. Section 6(c). 

5. No provision. 

6. Ten years and five years for inventory of sites. Section 4(a).

7. Projections of demand to the year 2000. Section 319 (b). 

8. No provision. 

What provision is made with regard to certification procedures? 

1. a. Each electric utility desiring to secure benefits of this legislation shall make public and file with the FPC a detailed construction proposal (one year in advance for facilities other than generating plants-two years for them). The proponent shall include a statement that the construction is consistent with regional plans and shall describe the steps he has taken to get approval from public agencies concerned with environmental factors. Section 403(a). b. If upon application by a proponent the FPC finds that failure to obtain approval of proposed facilities, as a result of disputes over environmental matters, will result in bulk power shortages, the FPC shall convene a Special Panel. 

Section 404(a). 

c. The Special Panel shall consist of one member named by the FPC and one by the CEQ. They may select the third member who shall be chairman. If they are unable to agree they shall request the President of the National Academy of 

Sciences to name the third member. Section 404(b)(1). d. The proponent must demonstrate that the construction is consistent with the regional plan, that he has made timely disclosure, and has made bona fide attempts to obtain approval from public agencies. Section 404(c). e. The Panel shall review all alternative proposals and select the one that best assures reliable power consistent with environmental consideration. It shall publish the proposal selected together with reasons therefor. Section 404 (c). 

2. a. Each State shall designate a certifying agency in accordance with guidelines to be prescribed by the President. Such guidelines shall establish criteria and procedures for selecting the best alternative plan or site from among those that have been suggested. Section 5(a). Section 9 b. The Governor of each State which designates a state agency must advise the Federal Certifying Agency (FCA). If the State agency meets Federal stand ards the FCA will issue a certificate of qualification. Section 5(b)

If no state body is designated, the FCA will carry out its functions. Section 5(c) c. Judicial review for denying or revoking certificate of qualification is provided. FCA findings must be supported by substantial evidence. Certiorari to 

Supreme Court. Section 5(e) 

d. Federal certificates (for federal plants) are obtained from the FCA.

Section 6(b) 

e. A utility may petition the FCA on showing that the State or regional certifying agency has not acted in a timely manner. The FPC shall prescribe rules regarding the facts necessary to constitute the basis of showing. If, after, consultation with interested Federal agencies, the FPC finds that bulk power reliability will be materially impaired as a result of the delay, the FCA shall have jurisdiction to act in the case. Section 6(d) 

f. If a certifying body finds for the proponent, after considering alternatives, it shall issue a certificate setting forth reasonable terms and conditions. The judgment of the certifying body will be conclusive on all environmental matters but it must make certain that all applicable Federal standards have been met. 

Section 7(a) 

3. a. In the event that the construction of bulk power facilities pursuant to a regional plan will be aided thereby, the utility and the council may request the FPC to assume jurisdiction to issue a certificate. Requirements that must be met are described in the Bill. Section 6 (a) and (b)

b. If the FPC determines that it has jurisdiction (order is appealable) it will convene a State Joint Board. Membership may be from 3 to 10 with no more than one member from each State and no more than half to the State members. 

Section 6(e) (1) 

State Joint Board can establish its own rules. Section 6(e) (2). 

When a decision is reached it is transmitted to the FPC with record of voting. If majority of State members disagree with majority of Federal members, decision shall be reviewed by the entire membership of the Commission. 

Under certain conditions new proceeding shall be conducted. Section 6 (e) (2) and (3). 

4. a. Each utility shall make public proposals one year in advance for facilities other than generating plants (two years for them). The proponent shall describe the facilities and supply copies to the public and to each agency that must review. Section 7(b) 

b. Proponent must certify to the commission that all reliability standards have been met and that all applicable State and Federal environmental regulations and standards have been met. FPC shall prescribe procedures. Section 7 (f) 

c. The FPC and the Advisory Panel on the Environment (see Section 12) shall review and comment on proposal.

After consultation with regional council, State commission and Advisory Panel, FPC shall issue comments. If FPC or Advisory 

Panel recommendations are not accepted the proponent shall submit to FPC a written statement giving reasons for rejection. Section 7(e) d. States are authorized and encouraged to regulate siting including those of federally owned facilities. Section 7(g) 

e. If there is a protest to the FPC that a public agency standard has not been met, the FPC shall transmit the protest to the responsible agency. The agency response shall be conclusive as to non-compliance. Section 7(h) 

5. a. Each utility shall submit for FPC approval any application for new construction in accordance with rules and regulations that the FPC shall prescribe. 

This requirement would be in addition to any State approvals which may be required for non-Federal plants. Section 410 (a) b. No construction shall take place for six months after publication of proposal in Federal Register or for as long any FPC suspense order is in effect. 

Whenever the FPC issues a suspense order it shall summarize the FPC's reasons. 

Section 410(b) 

c. FPC may suggest modifications of any proposal and if accepted by the utility and no objection is filed the proposal will be approved; otherwise formal hearings shall be held. Section 410(c) d. The Electric Power Environmental Council (EPEC) (see Section 405 (a)) 

shall review each coordination plant, each application under part 1 of the FPC Act and each proposal for construction approval, to ascertain if they are consistent with environmental considerations. Section 405 (b)

If not consistent, EPEC files an objection. Section 405 (c) 

Objection shall have the same effect as an FPC suspense order. Section 405 (f) 

6. a. Same as for 2 except that it provides for a State Joint Board in place of a Federal certifying agency where a state does not have a certifying body. 

Section 5(c) does not provide for Presidential guidelines for criteria and procedures for certifying bodies. 

7. No provisions. 

8. Same as 5 except it relates only to extra-high voltage lines. Section 409 (a) 

The utility must file two years in advance of construction. Section 409 (a). No provision for an Electric Power Environmental Council. 

What provision is made for judicial review of siting decisions? 

1. Judicial review is limited to determination that the special panel's failure to make evaluation of any alternative was capricious, arbitrary or an abuse of discretion. Certioriari to the Supreme Court. Section 404 (d) 

2. Decisions of Federal certifying agency subject to judicial review pursuant to Title 5 section 701-706 of U.S. Čode. Decisions of State or regional certifying agencies subject to review pursuant to applicable State law.

Section 15 

3. Judicial Review provisions of section 313 of Federal Power Act apply. 

(Appeal to appropriate U.S. Circuit Court of Appeals-Substantial evidence rule applies)-Section 14 4. Same as 3-Section 16 5. No provision. 

6. Same as 2.

Decisions of a State Joint Board subject to judicial review pur suant to Title 5 section 701-706 of U.S. Code. Section 14.
